Point Report by the Constitutional Committee on Amendment 155/A by Dr Josef Cap, Karlheinz Kopf, Members and colleagues on a Federal Act amending the Federal Ministry Act 1986 (Federal Ministry Act Novelle 2008) (39 d.B.)
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e6013	We're coming to the 1st. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First of all, Mr Magistrate. Stefan with a desired speaking time of 5 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e6041	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! The Federal Ministry Act regulates the division of topics between the individual ministries, and undoubtedly it is not crucial for what a government is doing and how the content is formed, but of course we find this in the government programme. We have already found out that the government programme gives the impression that you are trying to dive through as little as possible, just no waves as you say, and to process the whole as well as you can. These are the lessons of recent times. Nevertheless, it must also be noted that it is a symbol of how a government deals with things when it divides its ministries and distributes the topics to the individual departments. In particular, we have three criticisms: First: the new Federal Ministry of Economic Affairs, Family and Youth. On the one hand, the Minister of Economic Affairs is deprived of the competence for labour market policy and unemployment insurance, which goes to the Social Ministry, on the other hand, a new competence that has only to do with economic competence, namely for family and youth, is added. There's never been that before. Since 1920 the economic issues have always been located in their own ministry and without a further addition. That means it was always quite clear: This is an essential point of the Republic, which is to be dealt with in such a targeted way that it also receives its own ministry – without any other foreign subject. This time it's different. Especially today, given the current economic data, because of which the expectations for the next few years are very pessimistic, and we have to assume that economic growth is against zero, there may even be a slight development, especially today, where there are studies that the unemployment rate will increase by a total of 45 000 over the next two years, one would assume that the Minister of Economic Affairs and his ministry will have to focus entirely on the overall problem and not just two or three issues, all of which are very important, namely, the economy, the family and the youth. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In the family sector too, there would be a great need for action. We know that there is a disparity here, especially for multi-child families, that there are financial injustices to be overcome here. We know that birth rates are dramatically low and therefore all measures would have to be taken to improve here. To mix this now and to equip a minister with two and thus only two half competences is very problematic. We were and are still in favour of setting up our own family ministry. That would be an area that would deserve it. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Another point, the second point we are criticising: Research is divided into two ministries for unexplained reasons, namely the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology and the Federal Ministry of Science and Research. Why? – To reduce efficiency. I guess it can't be. This is a weakening of this area, which is why we reject it. Third point: the new Ministry of Sport and National Defense. – Even here it is quite clear, since there is a Ministry of Defence – or whatever it has said – it was its own competence, it was its own ministry that focused on it. The sport is now being added. I've been thinking about whether this is perhaps a recourse to Turnfather Jahn, because at the beginning of the turn movement, we've had an essential point. It may have been said that sport and national defence must be combined, but I do not assume that this historical knowledge exists here at all. If so, then perhaps not necessarily love to do so, so there must be another reason behind it. I can only imagine it this way: a minister, who is overwhelmed with his place of business, has negative reporting and is, in itself, very problematic as a minister; after all, in a conscience examination, he has declared that he cannot morally represent serving with the weapon in his hand – but now it can be morally represented to send soldiers into war. So a classic chuzpe! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In order to distract from this, it appears that it needs a positive report. And what is more beautiful than showing up with athletes? – In winter with the winter athletes who are still successful; it may not be so good with other sports in summer, but this time he will already overcome, he will already succeed in presenting himself well. – That’s definitely a humbug, we reject it, because that’s a mixture that doesn’t really make sense. It's true, there are athletes who are with the federal army, these are top athletes, but that's first only part of the top sport. Secondly, it is much more important to focus primarily on broadband and the development of the top athlete. The athletes do not develop with the German army, but they only come to the German army when they are top athletes. They grow up in clubs and schools, and there you have to put the weight. If you want to find a ministry for sport, then sports would have to settle in the fields of education or health, not in the defence of the state. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] That's enough reason to reject the law. Finally, I can say that I am very grateful to Martin Graf that he has remained so steadfast and has set a sign that left-wing human hatred and Stasi methods are not allowed to have any place in our Republic.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e6126	The next item is the speech by Mr Wittmann. 5 minutes desired speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e6147	Madam President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! Just to my previous speaker. The last sentence was really indispensable. Besides, he had nothing to do with matter. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Call the FPÖ: Necessary! Necessary!]] [[Further calls at the FPÖ.]] We're talking about the Federal Ministry Act. Of course you can see that like that. There will always be reasons for this scheme. There will be reasons that are against it. But there are two principles that go through in the design of this Federal Ministry Act: One is the principle of sparingness, and the other is the principle of synergies. Principle of sparingness: Two government posts have been saved. Of course, you can invent any ministries, because we have all imagination enough to develop new things, but it has to be saved in times like this. That is, I believe, the highest principle. And that's what happened to this Federal Ministry Act. Principle of synergies: We have also focused synergies on areas that belong together. Thus, the areas of work and social affairs have been reunited, because they have seen more synergies in this area than in other areas. That is, it is a reasonable solution to draw these agendas together. To the area of economics: It must already be noted that there will be an own State Secretary for Family and Youth in the Ministry of Economic Affairs. I believe that this too is a reasonable solution, that you have a shared approach here, but that is summarized under a ministry to be able to use the synergies here as well. The Ministry of State Defense and Sport will continue to be mentioned, which will also have a new division of competence. This ministry is also a very interesting one from the point of view of synergies, for the following reason: My previous speaker forgets that according to our constitution, sport is in itself a matter of country. This means that the legislative acts in the field of sports are essentially carried out in the countries. After the Second World War it was decided not to establish, so to speak, the state sport in Austria, as it is in other countries, but to choose the autonomy of sports. That is to say, the distribution of a large part of the funds available to the federal government lies in the autonomy of sports. A minister has no influence on this. This concerns mainly the means for broadband and those sports that form the basis. It remains a small part, namely the general promotion of sports, which is the responsibility of the Minister for Sport. And this general promotion of sports is essentially a support for top-level sports. If you have top sports in a ministry, it is not far from the Ministry of Defense, because the top athletes receive the greatest support in the social suspension by employment in the federal army. This means that the synergies are used here, too, by bringing together the range of funding for top-level sports and the social security of top-level sports, but the autonomy of sports is guaranteed in the distribution of the wide-ranging sports equipment that the BSO, the federal sports organisation, is responsible for. We therefore continue to have a separation between state and sport. This separation and autonomy of sport are one of those principles that actually constitute the essence of Austrian sport. It can be said that the BSO was one of the first NGOs after the Second World War. [[Abg. May. Molterer: Yes, Mr President.]] Without you knowing that there will be NGOs later, you've already presumed autonomy here. I believe that this intangibility – and that is undisputed across party borders – and that the distribution of funds for sport should be done autonomously, is a principle that the Austrian Republic and the development in sports have always done well. To conclude: I believe that this innovation ensures the economy, the use of synergies and efficient management. Finally, the success will depend on those working in these ministries. I believe that with Minister Darabos, we have a very sport-fine minister, who is also familiar with sports, he is himself one who does sports. [[Abg. Kickl: Who wants to lock up all right now!]] That's why I think the sport is well removed from him.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e6663	It has now become Mr. State Secretary Dr. Ostermayer to speak. I'll give it to him.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e6684	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! In the course of the coalition negotiations, the government parties agreed on the form of division of ministries and the allocation of competences, because they believe that this is a suitable basis for implementing the government programme. It is clear, as the honourable Member has already said, that there is a great deal of dispute about this. Molterer said. That was the case in the past. It has always been discussed which areas are best suited to which ministry. But this is not only an internal-Austrian issue, but it is also an international question that is being discussed. If you look at what the assignments are in other countries, you can see that there are very different variants. I have specifically looked at the example of sport. Sports is almost assigned in every state to another ministry. However, there is a country, which was mentioned several times yesterday in connection with the European Union, namely Switzerland, where there is a similar combination, as we are now proposing, where national defence and sport are combined. There have been several considerations as to how to change the Federal Ministry Act. One of the considerations was that, I say, the number of people on the government bank is being reduced in this way in relation to Mr Stadler. People usually see this as a reduction of the federal government. I know it is a reduction of only two State Secretarys, but at least a reduction in the number of members of the Federal Government. However, if the opposite had happened, I suppose there would have been a much more severe criticism. In this way, of course, it was not possible to fulfil what has now and also been called for in the Constitutional Committee on many occasions, namely that there should be Ministries of its own on individual issues. There is no doubt at all that the theme “sport”, the topic “environment”, the topic “family” or the topic “Youth” are very important issues, but it was nevertheless necessary to make a summary of issues if one wanted to reduce the government or to reduce the number of people on the government bank. And, of course, we have been thinking about each particular area, and we have given thought to what decision we should make. The second consideration was that one does not shift individual departments, but that, when shifts are made, as large areas as possible, which are already working together, are shifted. That is why we have moved the entire area of labour, namely labour law and the labour market, from the Ministry of Economic Affairs to the Ministry of Social Affairs and Sport from the Federal Chancellery to the Ministry of Defence. The area of family and youth came to the Ministry of Economic Affairs, where Mrs. State Secretary Marek serves as an additional member of the department on this subject, which shows how important this area is to us. This is also contrary to the opinion of Mr Stefan, who here said that the Minister of Economic Affairs is burdened with too many agendas. Many other points have been raised here, which would be worth mentioning, but in order to make it short, I will only take one point out, namely the field of'research'. After the government negotiations, an agreement was reached that basic research, which had so far been divided between the Ministry of Infrastructure and Research on the one hand and the Ministry of Science, on the other hand, was to fall entirely within the competence of the Ministry of Science with the FWF, the fund for the promotion of scientific research. With regard to the research applied, Mr Molterer asked the question: Has the division so far existed, namely, between the Ministry of Infrastructure and the Ministry of Research and, on the one hand, the Ministry of Economic Affairs? I say: Yes, she has proved herself! Then: Those who have sought research funding who have submitted projects have not recognized – yes, they have not been able to recognize! – that there were, as it were, two owners of the research promotion society, also because the cooperation between the previous Minister of Transport and Infrastructure, the current Federal Chancellor Faymann, and the then Minister of Economic Affairs, Mr Bartenstein, has worked very well in this area, thus fulfilling the principle of the one-stop shop. The issue of cooperation will also play a major role in the future – in the near future and probably also in the medium term – especially in the important areas of economics, job creation and economic recovery, and therefore labour law and the labour market will come to the Ministry of Social Affairs. I think it has already been shown in recent weeks that there is excellent cooperation between the Minister for Social Affairs and the Minister for Labour and Social Affairs, if this is decided in this way, and the Minister for Economic Affairs. I mention only the example of short-time work, where there was a very rapid and cooperative development of a concept as to how in the future such problems can be solved. As I said earlier: Of course, one can very intensively discuss which areas are to come or belong to. But the most important thing is that a government works together. This is the primary objective: to cooperate very intensively and constructively to deal with the problems ahead. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e6775	Madam President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Whenever the Federal Ministry Act is discussed, the same ritual takes place: While overall government posts are to be saved, special topics – which are undoubtedly important – are to be set up by their own ministries. As an example, Mr Stefan mentioned the family sector, which is undoubtedly very important. Mr Musiol highlighted the importance of the environment. The Greens would otherwise like to address the issue of “integration” and call for their own ministry, but that has not yet happened. For all these important topics, individual ministries are to be set up, but in total they are to be saved from the Federal Government. In order to be able to meet the demand for sparingness, it is important to summarize certain areas, as the Secretary of State has already said here, without having to devalue these areas. Nevertheless, all areas will be cared for with due attention to their importance. In the Federal Ministry Act, where the division of labour and the distribution of competence are defined, there is hardly any other subject of the famous and well-known principle: Everyone is an art that no one can do! There are just so many different viewpoints, and each of them has their good reasons, their special motivations, and there simply has to be a weighing. In any event, essential criteria are clear responsibilities to assign clear responsibilities, and that is the essential quality feature of the administration. The competence ball should be moved as little as possible between the resorts. It is clear that this cannot be avoided completely. The departments are to be arranged in such a way that relatives are cared for under one roof as possible in order to achieve as many synergies as possible and thus savings effects as possible. Citizens should not be confused as much as possible by division of competence. So the administration should be as simple as possible and also be coherent. It is very important that there is no conflict of interest in the same place of business that the appearance of this is already held back. That was what we had when the labour market agendas were assigned to the economic sector, and this lack has now finally been eliminated in this distribution of resources. All in all, it can be said that this Federal Ministerial Law was quite in line with the principles of efficiency, effectiveness, sparingness and transparency. And as I said, Everyone is an art that no one can do!

ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e7617	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! In principle, federal ministerial laws always deal with the question: What effect does a shift from one resort to another have? Mr Donabauer has explained that it is in fact all the meats where an area is located, but that it is about what is going to happen in it. That's always the classic question. However, we could have discussed the question of where the working days should be located in 2000 in this sense, and we could have left the area of work elsewhere at that time. But there was a different situation, and the SPÖ also criticized that at the time. So with shifts of resorts, a symbolic character is already set: Firstly, we are dealing with the question of what we want to express, and secondly with a certain political weighting associated with it. In this respect, it is, of course, a very positive aspect from our point of view that the working days are relocated to the Ministry of Social Affairs. However, I would like to deal with the topic of sport, after this has already been the main issue in the Committee, namely the fact that sport is now being established at the Ministry of Defence. I have a somewhat ambivalent attitude. In principle, I have little joy if this is a sign that it is military structures in sport, because in Austria, I think, the German army has relatively strong influence – perhaps less on sports policy, but on the sports structure. We know that there are many top athletes – especially in areas where there is not a lot of revenue – who could not exercise their sport without the federal army, and that it is almost necessary that, if you want to be active in certain sports areas, you have to find yourself in the federal army, make a career there, be federal army members in order to be able to practice sports. I think that you can think about it in principle, because in the end it is also tax money that is used for it, and I would like it better if there were not to be this necessity to go to the federal army – with all the acceptance of what the federal army is doing in part for sport. In this respect, it is already important that there is no fusion. So if it comes to the conclusion that what has so far been independent as a sports resort is, so to speak, only more connected with the German army, then that would certainly have serious consequences from my point of view. But I don't suppose that's planned. But what actually triggered the discussion in the Sports Committee was something different. That is what I think is less about where this area is going, but about the person who, I think, made two statements that were quite new – and where there is a great criticism of one of them – namely: On the one hand, on the issue of doping and criminal law and on the other, on the pooling of sport promotion. When the sport promotion is bundled, I can follow Darabos in large parts. We have a situation in Austria where the foundry can be the highest principle of promotion. This can be the same to a certain extent, but in top-level sports, I think, new considerations also need to be considered. If you compare Austria with other countries, you can see: With the successes in winter sports Austria is a power, in summer sports it already looks quite different. And there, I think, one has to rethink the funding structures, because it is necessary that targeted support is also possible here. There are countries that focus on top-level sport promotion: Sweden, for example, is a very successful country in summer sports, it de facto focuses on twelve sports areas, where it is really promoted, where structures are built, where there is also a role model, which then also leads to broadband. In Austria, on the other hand, there is a very wide scatter. There is, I think, really the right approach to say we need focus. There were also some criticisms about this. This is probably also because we have a structure in which the BSO is very strongly represented in Parliament, by President Wittmann, who of course has a small incompatibility here, because: There he has to preach the principle of foundry – otherwise he can hardly do it. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] We have the leading representatives of the umbrella organisations in Parliament – a double structure. So: As bound as the sports area is, I think, hardly any other. There are some kind of outside access that can hardly be operated freely. There are multiple dependencies – and therefore also difficulties in implementing something. But I hope that we can reach a consensus that, especially in summer sports, we should promote more specifically, that certain areas should also be better in the top sports sector, and that wide-ranging sport is also a topic to be devoted to. In this respect, the consideration was always: What about the Ministry of Education? – These agendas have not been given either. But that's why the theme that movement and sport have simply been pushed back and given much less opportunities cannot be done. It's supposed to be expanded again. Finally, on the subject of doping. – The debates will continue over the next few weeks. In our view, doping is a subject where a consistent political fight is necessary, namely to support sports in Austria, and where we have repeatedly felt that there is a certain resistance to a truly effective anti-doping law in the last revisions. We will also look at this in detail: how the control mechanisms actually work now, whether, for example, the bodies used for control in the Federal Chancellery really control or whether there are any weaknesses there. I think it's them. – In the case of individual legal provisions, there was still such resistance – partly from the associations – in the law-making process, which was not comprehensible. From my point of view, the proposal by Darabos to prosecute doping is something that should be discussed very thoroughly before you start somewhere, and where it cannot be a matter of putting the crime itself under criminal law – there is still this consensus in this House, as far as I know, as far as I am aware. However: The question of where there is a need to make persecution really possible in the case of doping is also re-examination – especially under the impression of the findings from the Kohl case – where the possibility is simply necessary to push really effectively on correct statements or to make statements at all in order to be able to take action. If there are athletes who say: I don't care, I'm being locked for two years; I don't need to make a statement, I get the lock anyway!, I think that's an unsatisfying state, in view of which we have to consider: What measures can be taken to clarify this situation? It can be difficult in the doping area for other reasons to break the position, it should keep everyone doping as he wants, because that would tie every sport together and it would be exploited so far that it would also be of concern to health. So we're for a doping fight, for effective anti-doping measures. And the question of putting the offense of doping under criminal law is something that we should be very serious about in this House.

Point Report of the Constitutional Committee on the Government Template (19D.B._Other Organiser Federal Act amending the Private TV Act and the Private Radio Act (40 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e8848	We're now on the 2nd. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Magistrate is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Stefan. Please, my colleague.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e8876	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! To the Private TV Act: An old liberal requirement that the dual broadcasting system can be installed. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: We have presented this to the government together!]] No question, an old liberal demand. That's exactly what I said, I didn't say anything else. Maybe that's a translation question. This now facilitates the advertising restrictions for private channels. One could say, of course, that the market regulates it itself and one can release it completely, so one can completely release advertising times, and one will see if the audience will follow it then. However, it has been concluded that there should be regulatory measures here, and therefore there are rules here. However, there is one point that is very disturbing to us in this regulation. So far, it was only possible to interrupt children's broadcasts only after a minimum of 45 minutes. However, this government template would provide for a 30-minute interruption of children's broadcasts. Why we want to introduce a deterioration in child protection is very questionable, and no one has found a real argument for it. With the argument that parents should pay attention to what their children are looking at, and that they should continue to switch to public-law channels, of course, one could even abolish any regulation and think – I said before – that the market is self-regulatory and the people should not let the children look at it. The fact is, of course: It is important to enforce child protection here as well. We have therefore also been looking for an international comparison and have seen that, for example, in Denmark and Sweden, children under the age of 12, there is no advertising in the children's broadcasts at all. There is also a significantly higher protection in the UK and Belgium in TV broadcasts for children. So we'd be strongly behind that. We have therefore drawn up an amendment which we are tabling here. The intention of this application is – to say it briefly, because it is usually more difficult to derive from the text of the law – that children's broadcasts can only be interrupted from a period of 45 minutes and can only be interrupted once. This means that there are many interruptions. Our proposal now reads as follows: It is Article 1 Z 1 in Section 36, where paragraph 3 is to read as follows: “Transmissions of television films (with the exception of series, series and documentary films), cinematographic films and news broadcasts may be interrupted once for each programmed period of 30 minutes for television advertising and teleshopping. The transmission of children's consignments may be interrupted at least once for each programmed period of at least 45 minutes, but only if the total duration of the consignment is more than 45 minutes in accordance with the transmission plan. However, the interruption may take place after 30 minutes at the earliest.” In an analogy, Article 2 Z 3 § 19 paragraph 6: “The transmission of religious services and missions of religious content must not be interrupted by advertising. Messages may be interrupted for any programmed period of at least 30 minutes at most once for advertising. The transmission of children's consignments may be interrupted at least once for each programmed period of at least 45 minutes, but only if the total duration of the consignment is more than 45 minutes according to the schedule. However, the interruption may take place after 30 minutes at the earliest.” Since we have to fear that, despite the fact that the government parties are genuinely factual and good arguments, without any polemics that apparently do not want to join, there is also a motion for a resolution from all parties; a toothless tiger, however, makes sense from the intention – that is why we will, of course, join him – that the Federal Government is asked to force the operators of private television and private radio stations to do no advertising interruptions in children's broadcasts. As I said, we will support this, but fear that this is a far weaker regime. We therefore also hope, in the sense of the new style which has already been discussed several times here in Parliament, that we will perhaps take on a truly meaningful and purely objective request from the opposition. It is of course to be feared, in the case of extension of commercial television advertising times, that, due to its financial crisis, the ORF is here a little suspicious and will say that it also wants advertising interruptions. It is true that we are undoubtedly in favour of the dual system, therefore also for the maintenance of public-law television, but first of all we need to remove the really massive problems, especially in the area of the use of money, the economical and meaningful use, but also in the field of compliance with the public contract. Only when these two points are actually regulated can you be satisfied with the ORF and with public broadcasting. But my colleague Johannes Hübner will do this in detail. At any rate, I ask you to join our request and thus give an increased priority to child protection.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e9003	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! This amendment to the Private TV Act is the implementation of the Audiovisual Media Services Directive, which has replaced the Television Directive. This directive contains a number of liberalisations that will apply in the field of television advertising and teleshopping. We already have the preferential adaptation to this directive on the agenda, because further adaptation to this directive will have to follow in order to secure Austria's location in international comparison, and because this is a relatively small location and, of course, if we prefer this liberalisation now, we will be able to enjoy these liberalisation directives more quickly and thus be more competitive and will also be able to hold Austria for international operators. We have new possibilities for interruption in this directive, which should ultimately facilitate the financing of the dual system and, of course, is also a commitment to the dual system. This possibility of interrupting children's broadcasts after 30 minutes is already available, and I consider this voluntary appeal to be quite effective, because this possibility has not been exploited until now, but the restriction has finally been respected until now, although it would have been possible otherwise. It's just something different now that you say you can choose within 30 minutes, when you do it, but still it must take more than 30 minutes now, so that a interruption is possible. So that means you've already taken into account that this was possible. I believe that this voluntary appeal will continue to be interpreted, because it has already been handled in this way. Much has really not changed in this context. But I think it is right to allow these interruptions. I also think it is right that one has a limitation in that one should not interfere with the context and that one also takes into account the nature and duration of the broadcast, when one makes these advertisements, so not arbitrary, but there should also be a connection. I also think it is right that this 20 per cent daily percentage has been lifted, and I believe that these measures can help to strengthen the dual system and ultimately also enable a variety of media coverage. We will therefore vote in favour of this regulation.

I now give an example of how this has affected and has also already worked out, namely that children's broadcasts should not be interrupted under 30 minutes, and if they last longer than 30 minutes – for example, 31 minutes – they may be interrupted once. Since there is a second regulation where the 30 minutes are mentioned again, that means that a second interruption may not take place until the next 30 minutes, i.e. 60 minutes in total, are exceeded. In that respect, there is a relatively clear system. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Zinggl.]] – This would mean two interruptions at 90 minutes, three interruptions starting at 91 minutes. [[Abg. Dr. Zinggl: So far we only have one!]] This is actually the continuation, just the legal shift that has been made in comparison to the current law. ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e10185	Ladies and gentlemen! Dear visitors to the gallery! As a Constitutional and Family Spokesman of the Greens, I would also like to speak about the interrupters or advertising programmes on children's films and children's broadcasts. In the Constitutional Committee, we were very quick to agree that children have a high, increased need for protection on the issue of advertising on children's broadcasts, only on how we want to give them this protection, there was disagreement. I am all the more pleased that Mr Brosz's initiative to submit a joint motion for a resolution on the voluntary restriction of private channels will be carried by all parties. However, I would like to introduce here a little more profoundly what science and research has done on this subject. I would like to highlight a special study, so to speak, because in the Constitutional Committee we have talked about branding and consumer behaviour, but what we have forgotten is that there are further effects of advertising, namely the effects on eating behaviour, on the health of our children. Scientists in the UK have played TV spots at Liverpool University for nine to eleven years, two different types of TV spots. In one group there were advertising spots for candy, in the other such for toys. The children had all kinds of treats when viewing cartoons that were interrupted by these advertising spots. The result is shocking. The evaluation showed that the children increased their consumption of sweets by up to 100 percent in the face of those advertising spots that advertised sweets. Especially children who already have eating problems, i.e. thick children, ate 101 percent more sweets when these interruptions came than when they did not come. In other words, we are required to be particularly careful in terms of the health of our children. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Especially amusing were the experiences of a number of colleagues in the Constitutional Committee – I am now deliberately saying “colleagues” – because it was mainly male colleagues – who have told from their lives how – under quotations – they “sacrifice” the wishes of their children – or their grandchildren, that was not expressed so precisely – when they had previously seen advertising spots. I believe that this study, which I have just quoted, shows that it is important not only to provide protection for the parent's wallets, but also for the children themselves in terms of their consumer and brand behaviour, in terms of their health. One aspect of the discussion was whether it was not the responsibility of the parents to intervene. Of course, parents bear responsibility, but it is precisely the responsibility of legislators to also create the framework conditions for protection. If we do not do so, we would probably be able to do so in all areas where we are setting up protection provisions for children. It's all the more important to be careful. I would like to finish with a test that I would like to carry out with you, which is not an advertising interruption. A test was carried out with children aged four to 13 years. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Scheibner.]] – You are welcome to participate, Mr Scheibner. I can tell you in advance: four to six-year-olds cut off 75 percent in this test – I tell you how precise it is – and twelve to 13-year-olds with 15 percent. I'm looking for you to complete the following advertising jiggle like these kids: Haribo – Make Greens Happy? [[Call at BZÖ: Make Greens happy!]] Makes children happy. Thank you.

Point Report of the Constitutional Committee on the Government Template (20D.B._Other Organiser Federal Law amending the Media Act (41 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e10363	We're now on the 3rd. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First of all, Mrs Lueger has spoken. Voluntary speaking time limit: 5 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e10391	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen of the House! This government template provides for an adjustment to the electronic age. There was a long, two-year discussion process, which was conducted with all the departments involved, including the Federal Ministry of Justice and the Federal Ministry of Education, Arts and Culture, as well as with the media owners, the Austrian Magazine Association, the main association of book trade, and above all with the Internet service providers, on which this government template was now created. It is only a further development of the existing system, where mandatory copies are provided for printing works and offline media, where in the future, however, mandatory delivery of online media to the Austrian National Library is also planned. It has been very careful to achieve a balanced balance. What do I mean by that? – It is intended to take into account the collective interest of libraries and the economic needs of media owners as well as a non-bureaucratic collection system so that no one is taxed in addition. At the same time, this regulation is intended to ensure that the transition to digital media does not bring together gaps, which in the future would mean that scientific work would be difficult, because certain things simply do not exist. At the same time, this proposal is also to be seen in line with the European Union's digitalisation strategy and the transition to digital libraries. The automated collection process is to be carried out with systems, so-called webcrawlers and webspiders, so that an increased effort is avoided and there is no additional financial and working burden on media owners. Only if, for technical reasons, it is simply not possible for the Austrian National Library to start its own work will media owners receive an invitation by which they are required to deliver the electronic media. Without a mandate, these need not become active by themselves. That is, one has really tried to keep the administrative effort as low as possible. In order to avoid a financial disadvantage to the media owners who deliver in paper form, the costs were covered with an absolute amount of €250. Everything that goes beyond that is borne by the Austrian National Library. The concerns of the Austrian Chamber of Commerce were also raised; the concerns were not only raised but also addressed. Young entrepreneurs and, above all, those media owners who have pure hobby media should not have to bear any cost in the first two years of their activity. To simplify the delivery system, it was agreed that only the Austrian National Library is responsible and then distributes its documents to all other collection libraries. As a protection mechanism for economic interests – just as we know it from other media – a so-called application for a restricted period is possible, where library users should not be able to view these media contents. Use options are regulated insofar as an agreement can also be reached that use should be made exclusively at the sites of the libraries. If today the government template receives a majority or unanimous agreement – in the Committee there was unanimous agreement – it should be approved by 1. ==References== My group will vote in favour of this government proposal.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e10455	The next speaker was Mr Obernoster. Voluntary speaking time limit: 4 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e10476	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! At the beginning, I would like to thank the government and the Committee for the preparation of the Federal Law, which amends the Media Act. My predecessor, Mr Lueger, has already reported this in detail: It is specifically about the further development of the existing regulation on mandatory copy law. Prints and electronic offline media are recorded, the online media are included. The aim is to ensure that no gaps arise between the Austrian National Library and the other collection libraries, especially in the university and state libraries. The Austrian National Library is to use the collection procedure automatically, thus avoiding the economic burden of media owners as far as possible. If technically impossible, media owners are obliged to participate in the delivery of their electronic media, but with a cost limitation. Hobby and young entrepreneurs are exempt from the costs in the first two years. The media owners have only one contact person with the Austrian National Library. As I said, I thank the Committee, thanks to the government. The ÖVP also fully supports this amendment to the Media Act, and we are particularly pleased that this amendment will probably be unanimously approved.

You've blended it a little bit elegantly, as if it were part of the procedure. The EIA procedure is under way, and my predecessor Josef Pröll has set up the consultative group, with Hungarian and Austrian colleagues, to avoid irritations with Hungary. There are six members: Hungarian State Secretary Oláh, representative of the Ministry, the mayor of Szentgotthárd, i.e. the affected city on the border with regard to the economic park in Heiligenkreuz, and the Austrian members are head of the section of Zahrer from my house, head of the department Holzer, also from my house, and the competent court council of the Burgenland government, Hombauer. This working group is about talking to Hungary outside the EIA procedure, where we can avoid irritations here, or perhaps talking about whether one can achieve synergies, for example that when this facility runs, the city of Szentgotthárd gets a district heating supply in this context. Hungary has shown interest. If something comes about, you'll see. It also comes to the exchange of business data, and I support this idea of Josef Pröll, that we offer Hungary full transparency and say how to achieve solutions together. To the project itself. – It is probably beyond dispute that a person who submits a project has a full right to rule of law, that a proper procedure should be carried out. For those who do not know: The aim of the project, RVH Restofstoffverwerbung GmbH, is to provide the industrial plant Lenzing Fibers with the heat from this residual recycling because it would otherwise have to be supplied with natural gas. The company says: We need more energy, thanks to God we can expand production. I say this – and I am aware of this problem – in an extremely structurally weak area, Jennersdorf, Güssing District, where it concerns jobs. But that cannot be the only argument I give you absolutely right, even from an environmental point of view. I am not a advocate of this project, I am in favour of a proper procedure. But the idea behind it has something, I think, to operate the operation instead of using natural gas, so to speak, with controlled waste – without valued the project. Especially in times when we are talking about renewable energy, it is already such that – especially here in Vienna, this is a good example – we can achieve energetic supply through combustion of waste by means of district heating. This is a goal where we also meet, namely to go towards an energy autarchy of Austria. – Not with this project here, but we have to take advantage of all the possibilities to become energy self-sufficient here, sometime; this is a beautiful vision. I like it very much, and I support it fully, in I know we need fossil energy sources, natural gas and oil, certainly for a while, but I think we're going to talk about this more often here. At any rate, I – and with this I would like to conclude – who comes from an area bordering Hungary, where we have a tradition of decades, to cooperate with Hungary at a friendly level, in order to achieve a new dynamic not only economically, but also culturally, following the disappearance of the Iron Curtain, according to the perspective of EU enlargement throughout the area. What the people live together – the language is a barrier there; I admire everyone who can Hungarian! – is it already a great perspective, and therefore I am very interested that Hungary is fully involved and informed; no question at all. In conclusion: For me, it is also crucial from the specialist that the environmental impact of this project is clearly demonstrated. - Thank you.

[[Interference of Federal Minister Dipl.-Ing. Berlakovich.]] - Steirer, too. Yes, Minister of State. That is to say, we now have this plant in the south of Burgenland, there are problems with the utilization of the Heiligenkreuz economic park, and now, ladies and gentlemen, this thermal waste recovery plant is coming. The foaming was obviously caused by leather factories in advance, by several factories. The foam went down. The Hungarians were very uncertain and even called on them to stop drinking beer from Austria out of protest and out of trouble about this foaming. Thanks be to God, the problem has been solved positively. But now we have to look at the project of this thermal waste recovery plant very cool, objective and sober. The dimension of this project is designed in such a way that the ten-fold amount of the waste that occurs in Burgenland can be used there. That is, a huge project for Burgenland conditions. It is also the case that about 70 percent of the amount of waste can be transported by rail to this thermal waste recovery plant. This means, however, that the three-fold amount of the waste that occurs throughout Burgenland cannot be delivered by rail, that the three-fold amount of the waste that affects the entire Burgenland must be delivered to this plant via trucks. This also means, of course, a great burden for this region, which means a load of fine dust, and these trucks will not necessarily be very quiet either. Something else is planned – and that also needs to be said – for the use of predictive sludges here. Then there will be this olfactory problem, which you have mentioned, also in this area, because of course these pre-explanation sludges smell awfully. Whoever has ever followed such a truck – there are containers in which this pre-explanation sludge is inside – knows that these containers are not dense. That means – whether by rail or by truck – there is a loss of liquid that comes to the ground, and this liquid stinks naturally. It is therefore to be understood that the population in this region is very, very skeptical of this project. I can understand that the people responsible for BEGAS are also looking at the financial aspect here and of course wanting to do a business, the business with the garbage, which is not at this moment, but is still a good business for a long time, but one must also see that this is a huge burden on this region. Now you are putting yourself in the position of a southern Burger country in a region that has been disadvantaged for many years, where there are still few jobs, which have not found a job at Lenzing Lyocell, you are moving into the situation of small and medium-sized enterprises, which also employ workers and who do not receive €700 000 in support per job. They are, of course, dissatisfied because one has the impression that one is now becoming a dungy of the whole region. The tenfold amount of waste will be burned there, only one tenth of it will be attacked in Burgenland. That is to say, it is not necessary to transport this waste from a distance to this thermal waste recovery plant. That is why I think it must be the better way to think small-scale here, not to take long distances, but to try to project smaller plants so that the waste from the region can be used there.

This now applies to the field of vocational training. There is a law on equal treatment under which lesbians and gays can no longer be discriminated against and complain when this happens in the field of the profession. Point First reading: Parliament's motion for a resolution (Doc. Alev Korun, colleagues and colleagues concerning a federal law that amends the establishment and residence law, the Asylum Act 2005 and the Foreign Police Act 2005 (Residence Law – 2008) (32/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e17316	We're on the 8th. Agenda item. First word message: Mrs Mag. Korun. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e17340	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, At the first meeting of the National Council after the election, we once again submitted our right to stay, our application for legislation on the creation of a right of residence or the implementation of the Constitutional Court's findings. The recognition of the Constitutional Court of summer 2008 is now known to all colleagues in the House, I assume once, at least to hear. The Austrian Constitutional Court has, in contrast to some political groups here in the House, recognized that there is a problem, namely that foreign citizens may not partially be able to exercise their fundamental right to family and private life if you do not have a legal procedure for a right to stay. This problem was identified by the Constitutional Court. That is why the Constitutional Court has repealed provisions of the current so-called foreign law and set a repair period. The Greens have been preparing and preparing a legislative proposal for a long time, which, even with this very poor, so-called foreign law, would ensure, through changes, that the fundamental right to family and private life is implemented. We are two groups in this proposal concerning the creation of a law enforcement law. Firstly, these are the so-called long-term asyl weavers and long-term asyl weavers. These are people who have applied for asylum many years ago and who, largely because of their refusal to do so, have not been granted asylum because of the Austrian asylum system. These people now live three, four, five, six, seven, some up to ten years here, have now taken root, have established families whose children are now going to school here. These are people who are partially or mostly very well integrated, where the children speak German perfectly, are successful in school, and are not allowed to take courses, for example, because the right to stay is lacking. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Jury.]] For example, to this group, the Constitutional Court said that it could not be that the right of humanitarian residence is an arbitrary act of the Minister of the Interior or the Minister of the Interior, that there must be a rule-of-law procedure for such a right of residence. The current Greens' right to stay would create this legal procedure, namely for asylum seekers and asylum seekers who have been in the asylum process for more than three years, who have participated in the asylum proceedings and have not become criminal. We demand, as the Constitutional Court has said, a legally standardised right of residence for these persons. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] The second group of stakeholders includes persons who have no right of residence for different reasons but also live in Austria for a long time, have taken root and are integrated here. These are some of the victims of the earlier alien laws, for example the Residence Act, which has led to an unprecedented wave of illegalization. By the way, a detail at the edge: The present draft of the Federal Minister of the Interior, Mrs Fekter, once again provides for such an illegalisation wave, which we are already warning against and which we are fighting, because it cannot be that legally living and settled people are losing their legal residence through curative deadlines and legislation. For this second group, namely for the illegalised and for people who are longer in Austria, we also demand access to the right of residence and a rule of law, saying: When, if not now, is the green leave application to be dealt with? – In any case, at the same time as Mrs Fekter's proposal for residence rights, which she will most likely amend. All those who read the evening edition of newspapers know that most of the national leaders have given this draft a departure. ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e18563	Next, Mr Katzian. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e18584	Ladies and gentlemen! My dear colleague, I am very grateful for the start of this discussion. I believe that we will have the opportunity to continue this discussion in the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy. It has been mentioned by you in the introduction to this debate a few things that I can fully understand and wherever I believe it is important that we deal with them in the political debate and in the political discourse – keyword: Changes in the working world, poverty despite work, and all the developments there are. However, where we are skeptical and where we do not believe that it is the solution to these problems, that is in the legal minimum wage. A debate is being held throughout Europe about the minimum wage. There are European countries that have implemented the minimum statutory wage and there are countries that have not implemented the minimum statutory wage – like Austria – but where other framework conditions can be found. If you draw comparisons in the discussion and look at England, you can see that there is a much lower collective contract coverage in England than in other countries. What the discussion about minimum wages in these countries has also brought about is that the adjustment of these minimum wages – and only when they are adjusted – has now been partially suspended in the crisis. There are examples in Europe where the adjustment of minimum wages has been suspended by the economic crisis or has been so low that the minimum wages can no longer achieve your desired effect. Another important reason why we are skeptical about this development is the fact that in Austria, we conclude over 600 collective agreements per year – as I mean, very good and in the interests of employees – that these collective agreements are very much in line with the interests and conditions of the respective industry, and that precisely this flexibility of different industry collective agreements also means that we can take full account of the situation in the sector as far as possible, in order to draw the right conclusions in the future. This means that our present system is also characterised by a very large flexibility. The effectiveness of minimum statutory wages also depends on how many employees are covered by collective contracts. I have already pointed out that in the UK the CV coverage rate is 13 percent. That is, 13 percent of employees are subject to a collective agreement at all. It is clear that there is a need for a minimum statutory wage. The debate in Germany, where there is no legal minimum wage, but very many political forces and also the trade unions that demand a minimum statutory wage, is taking place because the collective contract cover has now fallen below 50 percent and therefore there is a situation in which very many – even large – areas are no longer covered by a collective contract. I am therefore aware that the trade unions in Germany are making this call, and I can only support it accordingly. On the other hand, the collective contract cover in Austria is 95 percent, and we have many elements that do not mean a minimum statutory wage, but which are close to legal minimum standards and are at least very similar. On the one hand, collective agreements concluded in Austria are generally binding. – This means that they apply to all employees in an industry. Secondly, there are a number of other options that can be used accordingly to establish minimum income standards: whether it be the possibility of the statutes to impose the minimum wage rate or the possibility of applying local payment. For all these different possibilities there are appropriate legal instruments. These possibilities, these legal requirements are much better prepared and implemented in Austria than in most other European countries. On the 1 000 € minimum wage, I would like to say the following: It is true that there are still some areas where this has not yet been fully implemented. These are almost exclusively areas which do not fall within the competence of the Chamber of Commerce. Especially in the professions there are very difficult and tough conflicts. Recently, we have achieved a breakthrough with the lawyers – a long-term “championage”, you can almost say. There is already a collective agreement for the Vienna lawyers, and we are confident to close the gaps that still exist in Bälden.

On the one hand, it is about taking over the costs for the exploration and on the other hand also about a complete liability for damages, which arise, for example, on neighbouring property. I would also like to add that in addition to private property owners, who can really get into massive financial difficulties through such a situation, there are, of course, public property owners who are affected by this situation. In this case, for example, the city of Salzburg has been conducting proceedings against the federal government for years. The decisions on this procedure were different in each instance. At the moment, the matter between OGH and the Constitutional Court is being passed back and forth, because no one really considers himself responsible; it is not clear who is responsible. Finally, this whole dilemma points out that we do not have a clear legal regime. I think that such a scheme is really long overdue. This request is a solution. We had him – Mrs. Riener is now gone – once again in the Committee on the Interior, and at that time there was a debate in which representatives of all parties said it was an interesting approach or also a constructive approach, but the request was then postponed with the reference to a ministerial draft of the then Minister of the Interior Platter. The Künstler-Socialversicherungsfondsgesetz was innovated in the previous year and improved in important respects for the benefit of the artists, for example at income limits. The lower income limit could not be abolished for constitutional reasons, but an improvement was made by taking into account revenue from artistic activities such as prizes or scholarships. This will increase the lower limit for the entitlement to the benefit of the artist social security scheme. It has also been possible to take into account revenue from non-independent activities under poverty or to take into account the aliquotation of short-term artistic activity. Another improvement has taken place. This improvement resulted from the possibility to use the benefit from the Künstler-Socialversicherungsfonds not only for pension contributions but also for health insurance and accident insurance contributions. But the ceiling was also increased by the 2008 innovation, to just under €20 500. Thus, more artists than before are covered by this grant scheme. This has already been a progress towards what was given before. A success that is certainly due to the commitment of our Federal Minister Dr. Claudia Schmied and also to the consensus with the ÖVP cultural speaker Morak, who was found here. A compromise probably, but a significant improvement, which entered into force in 2008. Finally, a regulation of the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs imposed an increase of 20 percent in the contribution grant, from €1 026 to €1 230. So I'm sure we're making progress. There could be further improvements that have resulted in a clear rise in the social standard of artists. Your draft, ladies and gentlemen of the Greens, is not an improvement from our point of view, because it is too complicated because it is difficult to administer, your model is too contradictory and unforgettable in itself. It shows serious shortcomings, and that is not only our assessment, but it has also taken the Council of Culture. As social democrats, we will probably be able to use the better social policy for artists in the sense of a truly free art in Austria. In this sense, I am looking forward to the discussion in the committee responsible.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e20448	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! I must confess, this request surprises me. Amazingly, for example, I find the proposal that any party that decides an action plan to promote the participation of women in policy should receive a premium of €20 000, whereas in the event of 75 percent of the target objectives, only €10 000 should be paid. For the mere decision of an action plan to promote women, I think it is very generous to grant 20 000 €. The goal achievement is apparently less important. But I am even more amazed at the proposed change to the National Council electoral system. Consequently, all parties on their regional party lists, state party lists and federal party lists must have at least 40 percent of women among the candidates in order to be admitted to the National Council election at all. As you know, the active and passive right to vote are constitutionally anchored. In accordance with Article 26(1) of Regulation (EU) No 575/2013, the Commission shall adopt implementing acts in accordance with the procedure referred to in point (a) of the first subparagraph of this Article. 4 of the Federal Constitutional Law all men and women who own Austrian citizenship and the 19th. A year of life completed. If the admission of lists to the National Council election and thus the electability of the candidates listed there should suddenly also depend on the fact that at least 40 percent of candidates are women, this is more than worrying from a constitutional point of view. But it is also important to question the admissibility of such a strict quota system. Article 141(1) 4 of the EC Treaty allows for positive discrimination measures with a view to ensuring full equality between men and women in working life. Nevertheless, the European Court of Justice has a very critical position on the quota system. It assumes in constant judication that national regulations which give women absolute and absolute priority in the event of appointments or promotions go beyond promoting equal opportunities and are therefore illegal to European law. A quota scheme is only in accordance with European law if it contains an opening clause ensuring an objective assessment of applicants, each male and female candidate, so that the consideration of a male candidate is not excluded from the outset. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I should also like to remind you that in 2001 the Supreme Court qualified the regulation of the § 43 Federal Equal Treatment Act as non-Community law, because it automatically gives priority to applicants in the event of equal suitability, i.e. it does not contain an opening clause. I already know that this proposal does not refer to quotas in the field of working life. However, from the judicature referred to, a scale can very well be derived, on which quota schemes arranged outside working life should be measured. Finally, you also want to change club financing to reduce the funding if the women's rate of the club is below 50 percent. As you know, it's not the clubs that make the lists. The clubs are thus held responsible for something for which they can't do anything at all. To call someone responsible for something he is not at all responsible for, but I think that I am not only unresponsible, but also extremely worrying from a legal point of view. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Riepl: That's nice to say!]] Moreover, according to § 7 of the National Council's Rules of Procedure, the representatives of the same candidate party have only the right, but no obligation to join in a club. All of this is not taken into account. Such an unthinked request is by no means worthy of approval. Thank you.

Perhaps we have here too many officials or too many teachers in proportion to the total population? - Well, if You draw this conclusion everywhere, then we have to regulate everything, and it will be best if we write out what can come at all. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] In response to my criticism during the last legislative period that such applications ultimately only serve to intervene in the party bodies, the representatives of your party have told me: That's what you want! That's what I'm asking myself: What else does this have to do with democracy if one tries to intervene with a law in party bodies and then to say there: She may, she may, he may, he may! This really goes beyond democracy. That is the view that the Greens have of democracy! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] I am glad that you will not get through with such stupidities in this House. [[He-he-Rufe des Abg. May. Kogler.]] And I'm telling you quite honestly: Take care of real problems women have! Take care of culture! Take care of compulsions, forcibly, and for the murder of honor! Then you would help the women more than if you were to introduce quotas. I'll tell you something. After all, you of the Greens lost the last choice – even though you had the highest female rate.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e20617	The next item is Mrs Schenk's speech. I wish you every time: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e20639	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Let's end the tick war! I'm not available for a tick war. [[Congratulation and bravorage at BZÖ.]] The purpose of this proposal is to adopt measures to ensure that more women in political decision-making bodies, in particular the National Council, are able to participate in Gen. By means of a bonus malus system, a high female rate is to be rewarded in the national council clubs of the parties and a low female rate will lead to reductions in funding. In addition, there should be separate funding for parties deciding on an action plan to promote women, as well as for educational events aimed at increasing the political participation of women. Should this request, specifically the requirement of a bonus malus system, mean that, as long as there is no mandatory quota in policy and supervisory board, the proportion of women is to be bought? I don't think that makes sense. We are of the opinion that: Women should develop their self-confidence and not become so to speak divided. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] Of course, such developments as the increase in the proportion of women should grow – and they should grow faster. Even the new Minister for Women recently took back her quota requirement in the ‘Salzburger Nachrichten’, saying: A quota will not succeed from today to tomorrow, but will only be possible gradually together! – This is a better approach than that expressed in their speech on the occasion of the Declaration of Government. However, the action plan on women's promotion is to be welcomed. But for really committed politicians this is understood by itself. [[Demonstrative applause with BZÖ.]] In order to achieve or demand a reward of financial nature, it is naughty and will certainly not be blamed by the population. We want to save voters. We do not want this and do not represent this opinion either. [[New condolences with BZÖ.]] The women who can no longer afford daily life will not like it if the parties get money for a higher proportion of women in Parliament. If you look at the voter current analysis of the last National Council election, it is clear that the quota scheme has not been successful. The BZÖ has no female rate, but the BZÖ has chosen much more women than the green quota party – and that speaks for us! [[BZÖ's condolences.]] This shows that our policy is good and right and that we have addressed the right issues and have put the right solutions on the table. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] Only by removing inequalities in the areas of family, training and work will we ultimately achieve a sustainable improvement in the overall social situation of women and thus also a higher proportion of women in politics. And that's what we're trying to do!

ParlaMint-AT_2009-01-22-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00011_d7e20757	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen! The request made by the Green Group to bring more women into politics cannot win anything at all. Already the proposal to link the admission of political parties to a women's quota for the National Council election speaks for itself. Some party, who has been responsible for 50 years and more in this House, might, in your opinion, get the chair out of the door just because she may not have nominated a woman on the district list. And that's actually crazy! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] The proposal to make party funding and club promotion dependent on a quota is also legal and moral. [[Calls to the Greens.]] I say: Every Member in this House should be equally valuable not only in terms of his dignity and legal status, but also financially, without distinction according to his sex, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] I think that the introduction of quotas women in general is a snap idea, especially since women are considered incapable because you are given the right to reach positions and mandates only by means of legal requirements. [[demostratetive appeal to the FPÖ]] [[Mr Jawohl-Rufe and congratulations to the FPÖ as well as to the BZÖ Members.]] Where women appear strongly and with appropriate qualifications, they will prevail everywhere. [[New welcome to the FPÖ.]] By the way, we have quite different problems in the field of women's promotion. The current Court of Auditors report on Women’s Funding shows once again that women’s policy must be given some new choices. But apparently the same game we have long known from left-wing women's policy is played here again and again: Knowing that no results can be achieved on relevant questions is simply the subject of scam debates. To sum up, then, I have nothing else to say but that this proposal should not be made except that it should be placed in the circular storage – and that is what it belongs to!
